<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension at least three with strictly negative sectional curvature, we prove that normalized Liouville measure has maximal entropy for the unit-speed geodesic flow if and only if the metric is locally symmetric. This resolves Katok's entropy rigidity conjecture positively in these dimensions, including all rank-one symmetric types at arbitrary scale.

- **claim-main-entropy-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Suppose $n\ge3$ and every sectional curvature of $g$ is strictly negative. Then $$h_{m_L}(\phi_1)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\phi_1) \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \nabla^gR_g=0.$$ Equivalently, equality holds precisely when the universal Riemannian cover is a rank-one symmetric space of noncompact type, at an arbitrary overall scale.
  - Explanation: For closed strictly negatively curved manifolds of dimension at least three, Liouville entropy equals topological entropy exactly when the metric is locally symmetric.
- **claim-uniform-plaque-charts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-uniform-plaques)): The strong and weak stable and unstable foliations admit smooth local plaque charts on uniform geometric neighborhoods. At every fixed finite order, their chart and inverse norms are uniformly bounded, and their leaf derivatives depend continuously on the center.
  - Explanation: The Anosov foliations have smooth leaf charts of uniform size with uniformly bounded norms at each finite order and continuous leaf jets.
- **claim-plaque-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-plaque-contraction)): On uniformly sized local strong stable plaques, expressed in smooth plaque charts at the successive orbit points, every fixed positive derivative order of $f^j$ decays exponentially. The analogous statement holds for $f^{-j}$ on strong unstable plaques. On weak plaques in bounded flow boxes, all derivatives of the corresponding forward or backward maps are uniformly bounded at each fixed order.
  - Explanation: Every fixed positive derivative order of the iterates decays exponentially on strong stable plaques, and the analogous bounds hold backward and on weak plaques.
- **claim-entropy-conormal-forms** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-pre-entropy-forms)): The potential $-J$ has pressure zero and Liouville volume is an equilibrium state for it. If $h_{m_L}(\phi_1)=h_{\mathrm{top}}(\phi_1)=h$, there is a Hölder function $F$ with flow derivative satisfying $$\label{pre:cohomology} J=h+XF.$$ The function $F$ is smooth on weak unstable plaques, with uniform bounds at each finite leaf order. There are continuous horizontal $m$-forms $u,w$, with $u$ annihilating $E^u$ and $w$ annihilating $E^s$, such that $$\label{pre:volume-transport} \phi_t^*u=e^{-ht}u, \qquad \phi_t^*w=e^{ht}w, \qquad u\wedge w=c_*(d\alpha)^m, \quad c_*\ne0.$$ The form $u$ has smooth uniformly bounded finite jets on weak unstable plaques, and $w$ has the analogous property on weak stable plaques. These leaf jets vary continuously in uniform plaque charts.
  - Explanation: The negative unstable Jacobian potential has pressure zero with Liouville volume as equilibrium state; under entropy equality it is cohomologous to the constant entropy and yields transported conormal volume forms.
- **claim-smooth-splitting-implies-symmetric** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-smooth-rigidity)): If $E^s$ and $E^u$ are smooth on $SM$, then $(M,g)$ is locally symmetric.
  - Explanation: Global smoothness of the stable and unstable bundles forces local symmetry of the metric.
- **claim-raw-jet-dynamics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nf-raw-dynamics)): At every level $j$, the section map of $f$ induces a smooth invertible germ $G_{v,j}:\mathcal E_j(v)\to\mathcal E_j(fv)$ fixing zero. These maps commute with the projections $\pi_{j,j-1}$. Their base component depends only on $a$; their other components are polynomials in the hidden coordinates $(p_l,s_l)$ with coefficients smooth in $a$. For fixed $j$ their degrees are bounded independently of the length of a composition along the central orbit. The same statements hold for changes of adapted charts over the same stable plaque.* *The raw maps and their local inverses have uniformly bounded norms at every fixed order on suitable uniform raw domains. Let $-\chi_1,\ldots,-\chi_m<0$ be the stable Lyapunov exponents of $f$, with multiplicities, and put $\chi_*=\min_i\chi_i>0$. The derivative cocycle at zero has only negative exponents. In the quotient $\ker\pi_{j,j-1}$ they are sums of $j+1$ stable exponents on graph tensors and sums of $j$ stable exponents on log-density tensors. Thus their negative exponents define positive weights, each at least $\chi_*$, and the new weights at level $j$ are at least $j\chi_*$.
  - Explanation: At each level the section map induces an invertible polynomial-in-hidden-variables germ commuting with projections, with new weights bounded below proportionally to the level.
- **claim-invariant-subspace-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nf-blocks)): Suppose a measurable invertible linear cocycle has an invariant direct sum $E=\bigoplus_{i=1}^r E_i$, with distinct exponents $\lambda_1<\cdots<\lambda_r$, and each $E_i$ satisfies <a href="#nf:lyapunov-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nf:lyapunov-bounds">[nf:lyapunov-bounds]</a>. Every measurable invariant subspace $S$ satisfies, almost everywhere, $$S=\bigoplus_{i=1}^r(S\cap E_i).$$ No temperedness assumption on $S$ or on a measurable frame for $S$ is required. The statement also applies to a cocycle whose base transformation is $f^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: A measurable invariant subspace splits almost everywhere along the Lyapunov blocks, without any temperedness assumption on the subspace.
- **claim-homological-equation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nf-homological)): Let $L$ be an invertible finite-dimensional measurable cocycle with a single exponent $\gamma\ne0$ and estimates <a href="#nf:lyapunov-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nf:lyapunov-bounds">[nf:lyapunov-bounds]</a>. For a tempered measurable inhomogeneous term $e$, the recurrence $$\label{nf:recurrence} q_{n+1}=L_nq_n+e_{n+1}$$ has a unique tempered solution on every regular two-sided orbit. The solution is measurable. This conclusion also holds for the single-exponent triangular blocks described above.
  - Explanation: The nonresonant recurrence with tempered forcing has a unique measurable tempered solution along regular orbits.
- **claim-exact-normal-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nf-normalization)): For the raw towers in Lemma <a href="#nf:raw-dynamics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nf:raw-dynamics">6</a>, at every finite level there are measurable smooth germ coordinates $\Psi_{v,j}$, tangent to the identity, and weight-preserving polynomial diffeomorphisms $N_{v,j}$ such that $$\label{nf:conjugacy} \Psi_{fv,j}\circ G_{v,j}=N_{v,j}\circ\Psi_{v,j}.$$ Their linear part is the raw derivative, with its Oseledets grading. The coordinates and normal maps commute with the tower projections. For every fixed state level and every fixed derivative order, their norms and the corresponding inverse norms have measurable tempered bounds on measurable tempered positive radii. At a fixed level the coordinate change is smooth on a forward admissible domain; the estimates at different orders do not require one radius common to the entire infinite tower.
  - Explanation: Raw towers are conjugated by tempered smooth germ coordinates to weight-preserving polynomial normal maps compatible with tower projections.
- **claim-fiber-polynomiality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nf-fiber-analyticity)): At every fixed state level, the exact coordinate map $\Psi_{v,j}$ is polynomial in the raw hidden variables at fixed raw base $a$, with smooth coefficients in $a$. Write the normalized variables as $(z_0,\zeta)$, where $z_0$ is the level-zero coordinate. The local inverse of $\Psi_{v,j}$ is smooth in $z_0$ and real analytic in $\zeta$. Every fixed derivative in $z_0$ and $\zeta$ has this same real-analytic dependence on $\zeta$. The assertion makes no analyticity claim in the base or in the center $v$.
  - Explanation: Normal coordinates are polynomial in hidden variables at fixed base, and their inverse is smooth in the base and real analytic in the hidden variables.
- **claim-finite-weight-arrays** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nf-finite-weight)): In either normalized tower, the independent coordinates introduced at level $j$ have weights at least $j\chi_*$ for a fixed $\chi_*>0$. A prescribed upper weight bound consequently uses only finitely many independent tower coordinates and finitely many monomials. On the array of monomials of a fixed weight $d$, normal substitution has the single exponent $-d$, and inverse substitution has exponent $d$, with two-sided estimates and arbitrarily small exponential slack. These estimates continue to hold for finite tensor arrays and their invariant subquotients, with the corresponding sums and differences of weights.
  - Explanation: A weight cutoff involves only finitely many tower coordinates, and substitution on fixed-weight arrays has a single exponent with two-sided estimates.
- **claim-formal-affine-fields** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-main)): At every point of a conull invariant set there are formal affine fields $\mathcal A^u,\mathcal A^s$ of horizontal $m$-covectors with the following properties.* 1. *Their values contain $u$ and $w$, respectively. At the center, the exterior product of any element of $\mathcal A^u$ with any element of $\mathcal A^s$ equals the fixed nonzero exterior product $u\wedge w$.* 2. *The fields are equivariant under $f$, changes of section, and flip, with the same constant and height multipliers as $u,w$. In a flow box their height dependences are $e^{-h\tau}$ and $e^{h\tau}$.* 3. *On each entire typical strong stable or strong unstable leaf, every ambient Taylor coefficient has a smooth representative agreeing almost everywhere with the regular construction. These representatives are holonomic: differentiating along the leaf gives the corresponding higher ambient coefficients. The analogous assertion holds on typical weak leaves, using flow transport.* 4. *At every finite order the local representatives, their bounds, and positive radii can be chosen measurably. On compact plaque subboxes one may restrict to positive-measure families with uniform finite-order bounds. No common convergence radius for all orders is asserted.
  - Explanation: On a conull invariant set there are equivariant formal affine fields of horizontal covectors with constant cross pairing and smooth holonomic representatives along leaves, with measurable finite-order data.
- **claim-polynomial-recentering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-recenter)): Fix a finite state level. At sufficiently nearby regular centers on the same strong stable leaf, the changes between normal state coordinates are restrictions of polynomial automorphisms. They respect all lower state projections and carry corresponding actual states into one another. The same conclusion holds for regular centers related by a fixed flow translation, using the induced change of section.
  - Explanation: Changes of normal coordinates between nearby regular centers on a stable leaf are polynomial automorphisms respecting projections and actual states.
- **claim-regular-varieties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-regular-varieties)): After a conull refinement, all $V_j(v)$ are irreducible and smooth at zero. Their projections onto lower levels, including the full base, are submersions at zero. Nearby regular centers on the stable axis have their variety germs identified by Lemma <a href="#hull:recenter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hull:recenter">14</a>. These objects and all finite-dimensional ranks used below are measurable.
  - Explanation: After a conull refinement the Zariski closures of actual states are irreducible, smooth at zero, with submersive projections and measurable ranks.
- **claim-homogeneous-parameters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-parameters)): The smooth germs $V_j(v)$ have compatible positively weighted polynomial parametrizations. Independent parameters can be introduced successively with the state levels. Their new weights tend to infinity with the level. A finite collection of independent parameters extends to the full tower by setting all later independent parameters to zero. If their weights are at most $D_0$, a component of weight $d$ on this section has ordinary vanishing order at least $d/D_0$.
  - Explanation: The variety germs admit compatible positively weighted polynomial parametrizations with new weights growing with the level.
- **claim-pure-weight-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-evaluation)): Fix a finite level and monomial array. A vector $b$ in its actual evaluation span of pure weight $d$ can, along negative returns $-n$, be written as a linear combination of actual evaluations transported to the target center. For every $\epsilon>0$ the sum of absolute values of the coefficients is at most $C_\epsilon e^{(d+\epsilon)n}$. The transported states tend exponentially to zero. Arrays can be enlarged before making this choice, and the larger evaluation spans project surjectively onto the smaller ones, separately at each weight.
  - Explanation: Pure-weight vectors in an evaluation span are realized by combinations of transported actual evaluations with controlled coefficient growth.
- **claim-formal-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-identities)): If a smooth function vanishes on the local actual states, its Taylor series at a typical center vanishes on the formal germ of $V_j$. Moreover, let a function on a local smooth piece of $V_j$ be smooth in the base and analytic on each fiber. If it vanishes on the nearby typical actual states, it vanishes on a product neighborhood with connected fibers. The same assertion applies to each finite coefficient of a formal calculation having this regularity.
  - Explanation: Smooth identities among actual states propagate to the formal germ of the variety, and fiberwise analytic identities extend to product neighborhoods.
- **claim-opposite-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-pairing)): Every sufficiently small unstable alternative and stable alternative through the center have the same wedge as the actual pair. This remains true for their affine spans.
  - Explanation: Unstable and stable alternatives through the center have the same wedge as the actual pair, also on affine spans.
- **claim-finite-graph-calculus** (bound, [source](#stmt-hull-calculus)): Fix a weight cutoff $W$ and a target spatial derivative order $k$. There exist integers $r=r(W,k)$ and a constant $C=C(W,k)$ with the following property. Pushing a weight-truncated graph and log-density variation from time $-l$ to the target along the central unstable graph requires only source spatial derivatives through order $r$ at the contracted central preimage. Comparing two such inputs of tempered finite coefficient norm costs at most $e^{Cl+\epsilon l}$ in target $C^k$ norm. The constants $r,C$ are independent of the spatial Taylor degree used to specify the inputs.
  - Explanation: Pushing weight-truncated variations requires only a fixed finite number of source derivatives and costs a bounded exponential rate independent of the spatial Taylor degree.
- **claim-spatial-profiles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-profiles)): There is a parameter-formal family $\Phi_v(z,y)$ of graph and log-density records, with smooth coefficients for $y$ in a fixed small patch of the central unstable axis. Its Taylor series at $y=0$ is the prescribed tower of raw states. It is exactly equivariant, coefficient by coefficient, for the section dynamics and $z\mapsto N_vz$. For every fixed weight cutoff and spatial order its coefficient norms are tempered along the central orbit.
  - Explanation: A parameter-formal family of graph and density records with smooth spatial coefficients matches the tower jets, is equivariant, and has tempered coefficient norms.
- **claim-actual-plaque-approximation** (bound, [source](#stmt-hull-actual-approximation)): Fix a target norm consisting of graph derivatives through order $k+1$ and log-density derivatives through order $k$, and fix $K>0$. For a sufficiently large weight cutoff $W$ and a sufficiently high finite state level, the following holds. Start at a negative return $-n$ with an actual state in a bounded small forward-admissible domain, and transport its actual plaque to the target. On a fixed smaller central unstable patch, substituting its target normal coordinates into $\Phi_v$ truncated through weight $W$ approximates that plaque in the prescribed norm with error at most $C e^{-Kn}$. The level, cutoff, and constant may depend on $k,K$.
  - Explanation: For large cutoff and level, substituted truncated profiles approximate transported actual plaques with error decaying at any prescribed exponential rate.
- **claim-profile-translation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-transport)): At a nearby typical point of the central unstable plaque, re-center $\Phi_v(z,y)$ over that point’s stable axis. Its graph-and-density state series belongs formally, at every finite arrival level, to the arrival variety. The re-centered zero-order log-density has exactly the prescribed background value. This assertion is understood on finite homogeneous parameter sections and then in weight completion.
  - Explanation: Re-centering the formal family over a nearby point of the unstable plaque yields state series belonging formally to the arrival variety.
- **claim-constant-formal-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-rank)): The constrained augmented span is a free formal subbundle of rank $d$. Its coefficients can be taken smooth in the stable-axis variable, with their full coherent ambient jets. The resulting subbundle is independent of the hidden parametrization and of all higher-level lifts used in its construction.
  - Explanation: The constrained augmented span is a free formal subbundle of constant rank, independent of hidden parametrization and higher lifts.
- **claim-axis-coherence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-coherence)): The full ambient jets of $\mathcal A^u$ have smooth holonomic representatives along both entire typical strong axes. They agree with the regular construction almost everywhere and are compatible with dynamics, changes of section, and regular flow translations.
  - Explanation: Full ambient jets of the unstable affine field have smooth holonomic representatives along both entire typical strong axes, compatible with dynamics.
- **claim-measurable-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hull-measurability)): The construction and the representatives in Lemma <a href="#hull:coherence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hull:coherence">25</a> admit measurable finite-order norm and radius tests. Smooth flow transport supplies coherent representatives on typical weak plaques, agreeing leaf-almost everywhere with the regular data. For almost every individual strong or weak plaque, every fixed finite collection of coefficient norms is finite on each compactly contained plaque subpatch. Positive-measure families of these plaques can therefore be chosen with a common finite bound on the prescribed subpatches.
  - Explanation: The construction admits measurable finite-order norm and radius tests, so positive-measure plaque families have common finite bounds.
- **claim-signed-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-graded-contact)): Along almost every orbit of $f=\phi_T$ there are centered formal symplectic section coordinates, with tempered jets at every fixed order, having the following properties. The symplectic form is a constant form $\omega_0$. If $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{2m}$ are the signed Lyapunov exponents, counted with multiplicity and measured per iterate of $f$, then $$D=\sum_i\lambda_iq_i\partial_{q_i}$$ is symplectic and every normalized section map commutes with $D$. The coordinates lift strictly with $\theta_0=\tfrac12\iota_R\omega_0$, where $R=\sum_iq_i\partial_{q_i}$, and center height zero. The centered return map has the form $$\label{sym:graded-return} (q,\tau)\longmapsto(F(q),\tau+r(q)), \qquad r(0)=0,\quad Dr=0.$$ The actual strong stable and unstable Taylor germs are respectively the negative and positive coordinate axes, with their horizontal lifts.
  - Explanation: Along almost every orbit there are centered formal symplectic coordinates in which the section map commutes with the signed dilation and the strong germs are the coordinate axes.
- **claim-dilation-symmetry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-dilation)): In the coordinates of Lemma <a href="#sym:graded-contact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sym:graded-contact">27</a>, $$\label{sym:dilation-hamiltonian} H=-T+\theta_0(D),\qquad Z_H=D-T\partial_\tau$$ belongs to $\mathfrak k\subset\mathfrak h$. The operator $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}H$ on formal Hamiltonians is $D$ and is semisimple on every finite Taylor quotient, with real weights. Every closed $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}H$-invariant linear subspace admits the continuous projections onto each individual weight. On a finite-dimensional continuous invariant subquotient, weight-zero vectors have weight-zero lifts. Moreover $\mathfrak h_0\subset\mathfrak k$, and $\xi$ vanishes on every nonzero weight space.
  - Explanation: The dilation Hamiltonian lies in the isotropy algebra, acts semisimply with real weights, and weight projections exist on closed invariant subspaces.
- **claim-entropy-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-trace)): For every $p\in\mathfrak h_0$, the linear map $A_p=dY_p(0)$ preserves both actual signed tangent planes and $$\label{sym:geometric-trace} h\xi(p)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(A_p\|_{E^s}\bigr).$$ Here the strong tangent planes are identified with their projections to the section.
  - Explanation: For weight-zero symmetries the linearization preserves the signed planes and its trace on the stable plane equals entropy times the evaluation functional.
- **claim-full-projection-bundles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-full-projections)): Refine the regular set by a countable conull intersection. For every finite order $r$, the projections of $\mathfrak h$ and $\mathfrak k$ to the Hamiltonian $r$-jets have smooth extensions on each entire strong axis through a regular center. Their fibers at every point of that axis are the projections of the full formal solution spaces for the extended background jets there. Projections between successive jet orders are surjective smooth bundle maps. These statements also hold after appending a fixed finite list of linear jet conditions whose matrices are smooth along the axes and equivariant under the return maps.
  - Explanation: Finite-jet projections of the symmetry algebra and isotropy form smooth bundles along entire axes with surjective maps between successive orders.
- **claim-transitivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-transitivity)): At every regular center the map $$\mathfrak h\longrightarrow T_0\mathbb R^{2m},\qquad p\longmapsto Y_p(0)$$ is onto. It remains onto at every point of the smooth formal extensions on either entire typical axis.
  - Explanation: Evaluation of symmetry fields at the center is onto the section tangent space, also along the smooth axis extensions.
- **claim-effective-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-effective)): The open isotropy $\mathfrak k$ contains no nonzero ideal of $\mathfrak h$, and is exactly the coadjoint stabilizer of $\xi$. Evaluation identifies $$E=\mathfrak h/\mathfrak k\simeq T_0\mathbb R^{2m},\qquad \omega_E(\bar p,\bar q)=\xi(\{p,q\}).$$ This form is nondegenerate. On $E$, $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}H$ is $-D$, so it has no zero weight. With $E_+$ denoting its positive-weight subspace, the trace identity is $$\label{sym:algebra-trace} c\xi(p)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(p\|_{E_+}),\qquad p\in\mathfrak h_0,\qquad c=-h\ne0.$$ Here $p\|_{E_+}$ means the isotropy action by brackets. In particular $E_+$ corresponds to the geometrically stable plane.
  - Explanation: The isotropy contains no ideal, is the coadjoint stabilizer of the evaluation functional, and the quotient carries a nondegenerate form with the entropy trace identity.
- **claim-homogeneous-fields** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-homogeneous-fields)): Every $\mathfrak k$-invariant subspace $V\subset E$ has a unique $\mathfrak h$-invariant formal distribution with value $V$ at zero. The construction respects real structures, inclusions, and natural tensor operations. If $V=\mathfrak l/\mathfrak k$ for a subalgebra $\mathfrak l\supset\mathfrak k$, this distribution is involutive.
  - Explanation: Each isotropy-invariant subspace extends uniquely to an invariant formal distribution, involutive when it comes from a subalgebra.
- **claim-axis-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sym-axis-transport)): On either entire typical strong axis there are local smooth formal identifications of $(\mathfrak h,\mathfrak k,\xi)$, induced by strict contact coordinate changes. They may be chosen smoothly for a smooth finite-dimensional family of paths on an axis patch. Every intrinsic isotropy-invariant tensor or subspace construction, extended homogeneously as in Proposition <a href="#sym:homogeneous-fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sym:homogeneous-fields">33</a>, has smooth full ambient formal jets on that axis, and these jets are holonomic there. The same conclusion holds for a continuous choice from a finite intrinsic list of invariant subspaces after these identifications. Natural choices commute with the dynamics. Flow translation gives the corresponding weak-axis statement, with almost-everywhere agreement at regular centers.
  - Explanation: Strict contact changes identify the symmetry data along axes, so intrinsic invariant constructions have smooth holonomic jets there.
- **claim-coisotropic-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-coisotropic)): The set $\mathfrak l$ is an open intrinsic subalgebra containing $\mathfrak k$ and $H$, and has arbitrarily small open ideals for its induced topology. The subspace $L=\mathfrak l/\mathfrak k$ of $E$ is coisotropic, meaning $L^\omega\subset L$. If $\mathfrak h$ is infinite dimensional, then $\mathfrak l\ne\mathfrak h$.
  - Explanation: The equicontinuity subalgebra is open and intrinsic, its image in the transverse space is coisotropic, and it is proper in the infinite-dimensional case.
- **claim-minimal-ideals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-minimal-ideals)): The algebra $\mathfrak h$ has no nonzero closed abelian ideal. Every nonzero closed ideal contains a minimal nonzero closed ideal. There are finitely many such minimal ideals, each is topologically perfect, and their common centralizer in $\mathfrak h$ is zero.
  - Explanation: The symmetry algebra has no nonzero closed abelian ideal and finitely many topologically perfect minimal ideals with trivial common centralizer.
- **claim-derivation-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-derivation-realization)): For an ideal <a href="#alg:current-ideal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:current-ideal">[alg:current-ideal]</a>, every continuous derivation has a unique decomposition into a formal parameter vector field and an $\mathcal O_e$-family of continuous derivations of $S$: $$\mathop{\mathrm{Der}}(I)=W_e\otimes1+ \mathcal O_e\widehat\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Der}}(S). \label{alg:current-derivations}$$ The action of $\mathfrak h$ on its finitely many minimal ideals gives a faithful topological embedding with closed image in the product of these derivation algebras.
  - Explanation: Continuous derivations of a current ideal split into parameter vector fields and families of factor derivations, giving a faithful embedding of the algebra.
- **claim-translation-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-translation-test)): Let $A$ be a normalizing formal vector field of an infinite simple Cartan algebra, and suppose that it does not fix the origin. Its lowest nonzero grade is $-b<0$. The grade $-b$ part of $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}A$ maps each homogeneous component of grade $j+b$ onto the component of grade $j$. Consequently the family $\{(\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}A)^n:n\ge0\}$ is not equicontinuous for the formal linear topology.
  - Explanation: A normalizing field not fixing the origin lowers grade onto lower components, so its adjoint powers are not equicontinuous.
- **claim-origin-stabilizers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-intrinsic-l)): The set $\mathfrak l$ is the intersection of the subalgebras $\mathfrak l_i$. In particular it is open and intrinsic, contains $H$, and has arbitrarily small open ideals for its induced topology.
  - Explanation: The equicontinuity subalgebra coincides with the intersection of origin stabilizers over the minimal ideals.
- **claim-characteristic-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-characteristic-representation)): We have $\mathfrak u/\mathfrak k=P$, and symplectic pairing identifies $P$ with $B^*$ as a $\mathfrak k$-module. Transferring the dual bracket representation on $B^*$ gives a representation of all of $\mathfrak l$ on $P$ extending this $\mathfrak k$ action.
  - Explanation: The characteristic space is dual to the base tangent space and carries a representation of the whole subalgebra extending the isotropy action.
- **claim-pure-sign-isotypic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-signs)): If $B\ne0$, let $$P'=\operatorname{soc}_{\mathfrak l}(B^*)\subset P \label{alg:socle}$$ under the transferred representation of Lemma <a href="#alg:characteristic-representation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:characteristic-representation">40</a>; the socle is the sum of all irreducible submodules. It is nonzero. Its two $H$-sign parts are sums of full $\mathfrak u$-isotypic components, and in particular are invariant under the full transferred $\mathfrak u$ action.* *If $\mathfrak h$ is finite dimensional and $\mathfrak l=\mathfrak h$, then $E$ is a semisimple $\mathfrak k$-module whose $H$-sign parts are sums of full isotypic components. In either case there are only finitely many possible sums of isotypic components in the module under consideration.
  - Explanation: Sign parts of the socle of the dual representation are sums of full isotypic components, so only finitely many choices occur.
- **claim-radical-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-radical-action)): Let $g$ be a finite-dimensional complex Lie algebra with solvable radical $r$. On every irreducible finite-dimensional $g$-module, $r$ acts by scalars and $[g,r]$ acts trivially. On every finite-dimensional $g$-module each element of $[g,r]$ acts nilpotently.
  - Explanation: On finite-dimensional modules the radical acts by scalars on irreducibles and its commutator with the algebra acts nilpotently.
- **claim-finite-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-finite-trace)): For $p\in\mathfrak l_0$, identity <a href="#alg:entropy-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:entropy-trace">[alg:entropy-trace]</a> becomes $$\begin{split} c\,\xi(p) ={}&\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(p\|(\mathfrak l/\mathfrak u)_+\bigr) +\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(p\|B_+\bigr) -\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(p\|B_-\bigr). \end{split} \label{alg:three-traces}$$ The functional on $g$ annihilates $[g,r_g]$, where $r_g$ is the radical of $g$.
  - Explanation: The entropy trace splits across a three-step filtration and the functional vanishes on the commutator with the radical of the finite quotient.
- **claim-reductive-stabilizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-reductive-stabilizer)): The algebra $\mathfrak u'$ is reductive. Its center on the semisimple summand of $g'$ is toral. The restriction to $\mathfrak u'$ of every irreducible $g'$-module is semisimple, and $g'/\mathfrak u'$ is a semisimple $\mathfrak u'$-module.
  - Explanation: The stabilizer in the reductive quotient is reductive with toral center and acts semisimply on irreducible modules and on the quotient.
- **claim-algebraic-axis-coherence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-axis-coherence)): The planes and subalgebras above are measurable on a common conull set and commute with the dynamics and real conjugation. Their invariant homogeneous formal fields have full ambient jets smooth and holonomic along both entire typical strong axes, with the weak versions obtained by flow transport.* *In the infinite case $P'$ is flip-invariant. Its actual stable and unstable parts $$P^s=P'\cap E^s, \qquad P^u=P'\cap E^u$$ are both nonzero, have the same rank almost everywhere, and have the stated coherent formal extensions. They remain invariant under the full transferred $\mathfrak u$ action. In the finite case the formal sign fields have center values exactly $E^s$ and $E^u$. All these formal fields are invariant under the signed dilation.
  - Explanation: The algebraic planes are measurable and dynamics-compatible, and their invariant formal fields are smooth and holonomic along entire axes, with nonzero stable and unstable parts.
- **claim-algebraic-dichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-alg-dichotomy)): At typical centers exactly one of the following alternatives holds, with the same alternative almost everywhere.* 1. *The symmetry algebra is finite dimensional. There are $\mathfrak h$-invariant formal fields with center values $E^s$ and $E^u$, obtained from finite isotypic sign choices. Their full ambient jets are smooth and holonomic along both entire typical axes and equivariant under the dynamics.* 2. *The symmetry algebra is infinite dimensional. The intrinsic proper open subalgebra $\mathfrak l\supset\mathfrak k$ determines an involutive coisotropic formal distribution $L$, whose characteristic distribution is $P=L^\omega$. It contains an intrinsic nonzero plane $P'$ with nonzero actual stable and unstable parts $P^s,P^u$. These parts are invariant under the full $\mathfrak u$ action transferred from $B^*$, and all these formal fields have the same whole-axis coherence and equivariance.
  - Explanation: Exactly one of a finite-dimensional alternative with fields valued in the stable and unstable planes, or an infinite-dimensional alternative with a coisotropic distribution, holds almost everywhere.
- **claim-formal-affine-connection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-formal-affine)): The formal characteristic leaves of $P$ carry a canonical flat torsion-free connection. Their horizontal lifts to $\ker\alpha$ are integrable. The lifted fields $P^s,P^u$ are parallel along these leaves. The horizontal affine exponential $$\mathcal E_a(V),\qquad V\in P_a,$$ including its height coordinate, is a formal map equivariant under $f$. Its finite jets have the whole-axis coherence and measurability properties stated above. Restricted to $P^s_a$, respectively $P^u_a$, its Taylor series lies in the actual central strong stable, respectively strong unstable, plaque germ.
  - Explanation: Characteristic leaves carry a canonical flat torsion-free connection for which the sign fields are parallel, with an equivariant formal exponential into the actual plaque germs.
- **claim-stable-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-stable-restriction)): On an entire typical strong stable leaf, $P^s$ is a genuine smooth involutive subbundle, with a genuine flat torsion-free connection on its integral slices. The bundle $P^u$ has a smooth flat partial connection along those slices, induced by the characteristic connection. Locally one can choose smooth frames of $P^u$ that are parallel on every $P^s$ slice and depend smoothly on the transverse slice label. The analogous assertions hold with the signs exchanged. All these actual structures are equivariant on transported axis patches.
  - Explanation: On an entire stable leaf the stable sign field is a genuine smooth involutive subbundle with a flat torsion-free connection and parallel frames of the unstable one.
- **claim-local-stable-exponential** (bound, [source](#stmt-real-local-stable-exponential)): There is a positive-measure set $K$ of regular centers and constants $\eta_0,c_1,c_2>0$ such that, for $a\in K$ and $x\in P^s_a$ with $\|x\|\le\eta_0$, the affine geodesic with initial velocity $x$ exists through time $1$, stays in a fixed geometric stable plaque, and satisfies $$\label{real:local-comparison} c_1\|x\|\le d_s(a,\operatorname{Exp}^s_a x)\le c_2\|x\|.$$
  - Explanation: On a positive-measure set of centers the affine geodesics of small initial velocity exist for unit time and compare linearly with intrinsic stable distance.
- **claim-return-gaps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-returns)): If an increasing sequence of positive integers $(n_i)$ has a positive asymptotic frequency, then $$n_i-n_{i-1}=o(n_i).$$ In particular this holds for the successive visits of almost every point to the set $K$ of Lemma <a href="#real:local-stable-exponential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="real:local-stable-exponential">49</a>.
  - Explanation: Visit times with positive frequency have gaps that are small relative to the time itself.
- **claim-proper-exponential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-proper-exponential)): At a typical center $a$, the affine exponential $$\operatorname{Exp}^s_a:P^s_a\longrightarrow W^s(a)$$ is defined for every initial vector. It is smooth, and it leaves every compact subset of the intrinsic stable leaf as $\|x\|\to\infty$. In particular a nonzero initial vector determines a complete proper forward affine ray in that stable leaf.
  - Explanation: At a typical center the affine exponential is defined on all vectors, smooth and proper, giving complete proper rays in the stable leaf.
- **claim-iterate-derivative-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-real-iterate-bounds)): Forward iterates restricted to uniform local strong stable plaques have uniformly bounded derivatives of every fixed order. The same holds on weak stable plaques with a bounded local flow coordinate. On the ambient manifold, for every fixed $j$ there are constants $C_j,K_j$ such that $$\\|f^r\\|_{C^j}+\\|f^{-r}\\|_{C^j}\le C_j e^{K_jr} \qquad(r\ge0)$$ in bounded local charts, with the zeroth-order position term omitted. If $D_0\Subset D_1$ are patches in a strong stable leaf, the sets $f^{-r}D_0$ have uniform positive intrinsic room inside $f^{-r}D_1$.
  - Explanation: Forward iterates have uniformly bounded derivatives on stable plaques and at most exponentially growing ambient derivatives at each fixed order.
- **claim-product-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-real-product-derivatives)): Consider a product of finite matrices of a fixed size, $$A_{r-1}(a)\cdots A_0(a),$$ on uniform plaque charts. Suppose $\\|A_i\\|\le M$, with $M\ge1$, and that $\\|D^j A_i\\|\le C_j$ for every fixed $j$, uniformly in $i,r$. Then $$\bigl\\|D^j(A_{r-1}\cdots A_0)\bigr\\| \le C'_j(r+1)^j M^r \le C''_j e^{(\log M+1)r}.$$ The exponential rate is independent of $j$. The same conclusion holds with a terminal vector of smooth data having bounded derivatives of every fixed order.
  - Explanation: Derivatives of long matrix products grow at an exponential rate set by the base norm and independent of the derivative order.
- **claim-interior-interpolation** (bound, [source](#stmt-real-interpolation)): On uniform coordinate balls with a fixed amount of interior room, for integers $0<j<r$, $$\label{real:interpolation-inequality} \\|D^j a\\|_{C^0} \le C_{j,r}\left( \\|a\\|_{C^0}^{1-j/r}\\|a\\|_{C^r}^{j/r} +\\|a\\|_{C^0}\right).$$ In particular, if $\\|a_n\\|_{C^0}\le C e^{-\beta n}$ and $\\|a_n\\|_{C^r}\le C_r e^{A n}$, then its $j$th derivatives are bounded by a constant times $$e^{-\{\beta(1-j/r)-Aj/r\}n}+e^{-\beta n}.$$ The statement holds componentwise for tensors in uniformly bounded frames.
  - Explanation: Intermediate derivatives on balls with interior room are bounded by an interpolation between the sup norm and a higher-order norm.
- **claim-good-points** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-good-points)): Fix an integer $k$ and a sufficiently small geometric radius $\rho>0$. After a conull refinement, there is a constant $B_k$ such that every intrinsic stable disk of radius $\rho$ in each entire typical stable leaf contains a regular point at which the jets of $\mathcal E$ through order $k$ have norm at most $B_k$. The refinement can be imposed simultaneously for all $k$ and all integer iterates.
  - Explanation: Every stable disk of fixed small radius in a typical leaf contains a regular point with uniformly bounded jets up to any fixed order.
- **claim-naive-jet-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-real-naive)): For each fixed $k$ there is $A_k\ge0$, independent of the stable derivative order $j$, such that $$\label{real:naive-estimate} \\|D_s^jG_{n,k}\\|\le C_{j,k}e^{A_kn} \qquad(j\ge0)$$ on the moving patches, in uniform stable charts.
  - Explanation: Pulled-back jet coefficients have exponential bounds whose rate does not depend on the stable derivative order.
- **claim-jet-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-real-decay)): There exists $c_0>0$, independent of the fixed orders $j,k$, such that $$\label{real:decay-estimate} \\|D_s^jG_{n,k}\\|\le C_{j,k}e^{-c_0kn} \qquad(k\ge1,\ j\ge0)$$ on $f^{-n}D$. Constants may depend on the initial patches and the frame $Y$. No bound uniform in $j$ or $k$ is asserted for the prefactors.
  - Explanation: Pulled-back parameter jets decay exponentially at a rate proportional to the degree, with a constant independent of the fixed orders.
- **claim-actualization** (construction, [source](#stmt-real-actualization)): For the patches and frame in <a href="#real:pulled-jet" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="real:pulled-jet">[real:pulled-jet]</a>, there is a smooth map $$F:D\times\mathbb R^{d_u}\longrightarrow S\widetilde M$$ whose Taylor series in $y$ at zero is $\mathcal F$, such that $$F(a,0)=a,\qquad D_yF(a,0)=Y(a),\qquad F(a,y)\in W^u(a).$$ The construction depends only on the initial vector $Y(a)y$ and is compatible on overlapping patches and under $f$.* *More precisely, put $$V_{n,R}(s,y)=\sum_{k=1}^R G_{n,k}(s)[y^{\otimes k}], \qquad F_{n,R}(a,y)=f^n\exp_{f^{-n}a}V_{n,R}(f^{-n}a,y).$$ On every compact parameter set and compactly contained base patch, the following holds. For every fixed $j$ and $L>0$, all sufficiently large fixed $R$ give $$\label{real:fast-approximation} \\|F-F_{n,R}\\|_{C^j}\le C_{R,j,L}e^{-Ln},$$ and, after applying $f^{-n}$ and taking the logarithm based at $f^{-n}a$, $$\label{real:fast-backward} \left\\|\log_{f^{-n}a}(f^{-n}F(a,y)) -V_{n,R}(f^{-n}a,y)\right\\|_{C^j(a,y)} \le C_{R,j,L}e^{-Ln}.$$ Norms comparing nearby points use common bounded charts.
  - Explanation: Finite truncations converge to a smooth map realizing the formal jet and lying in the unstable leaf, with fast approximation estimates.
- **claim-normal-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-normal-extension)): Let $Q$ be the formal projection onto the perpendicular of $P^s$ for a smooth background metric. At regular centers, and on their entire typical unstable axes, use its smooth coherent coefficient extensions. For each fixed pair of integers $N,r$, one can choose a positive-measure set of centers with the following uniform property: on a fixed small unstable plaque through the center, there is a smooth ambient matrix field $\widehat Q$ which equals the actual annihilating projection on the plaque, has the prescribed ambient Taylor jet through order $N$ at the center, and has uniformly bounded $C^r$ norm in a neighborhood of that plaque.
  - Explanation: The normal projection extends to a smooth ambient matrix field with prescribed jet and uniform bounds on a positive-measure set of centers.
- **claim-stable-tangency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-stable-tangency)): Suppose the frame $Y$ is parallel along the $P^s$ slices of the base patch. Then, for every finite $y$, $$\label{real:rectangle-tangency} D_aF(a,y)[P^s_a]\subset E^s_{F(a,y)}.$$ Consequently a path obtained by translating a $P^s$-tangent base path by a parallel $P^u$ input lies in one actual strong stable leaf, while each of its points lies in the strong unstable leaf of the corresponding base point.
  - Explanation: Translating stable-tangent base paths by a parallel unstable input gives paths tangent to the strong stable bundle, lying in a single strong stable leaf.
- **claim-infinite-alternative-excluded** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-infinite-excluded)): The infinite-dimensional alternative of Theorem <a href="#alg:dichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:dichotomy">46</a> cannot occur.
  - Explanation: The infinite-dimensional alternative leads to a contradiction with Busemann divergence at the ideal boundary and cannot occur.
- **claim-formal-tangency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-smooth-tangency)): In the finite alternative, the positive-$D$ invariant formal plane field is tangent to the entire formal central unstable plaque. Consequently, on each entire typical strong stable leaf, all finite Taylor jets of the actual weak unstable plaques are smooth functions of their crossing point. They have holonomic full formal extensions there. The same statements hold on the weak stable leaf generated by that strong stable leaf.
  - Explanation: In the finite alternative the invariant formal plane is tangent to the central unstable plaque, so actual plaque jets depend smoothly on crossing points.
- **claim-formal-labels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-smooth-formal-labels)): The function $q_0$ has a distinguished full formal ambient extension along $\mathcal B$. Its coefficients are smooth and holonomic on $\mathcal B$, it is formally constant on every actual weak unstable plaque crossing $\mathcal B$, and its finite jets transform by scalar pullback under the flow. These assertions concern formal jets along $\mathcal B$, and do not assert differentiability of $q$ elsewhere.
  - Explanation: The leaf label has a formal ambient extension along the weak stable axis with smooth holonomic coefficients, constant on plaques and flow invariant.
- **claim-backward-geometry** (bound, [source](#stmt-smooth-backward-geometry)): Let $K\Subset\mathcal U$. There are $b>0$, $C<\infty$, and a compact $K_{\mathcal B}\Subset\mathcal B$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$ and $v\in K$, $$\|t(f^{-n}v)\|\leq Ce^{-bn},\qquad f^n\rho(f^{-n}v)\in K_{\mathcal B}.$$ The actual weak unstable plaque through $f^{-n}v$ crosses the axis $t=0$ at the same Busemann height, at a point $a_n(v)$. That crossing and the axis points underlying the intervening local plaque graph also have their $f^n$-images in $K_{\mathcal B}$, after enlarging this compact set. All assertions hold on a fixed neighborhood of $K$.
  - Explanation: Backward iterates of compact sets approach the fixed-future axis exponentially in angular coordinates while staying over a compact axis set.
- **claim-axis-derivative-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-smooth-naive)): For each angular degree $l$ there is $A_l<\infty$ such that, for every $j\geq0$ and every $L\Subset\mathcal B$, $$\label{smooth:naive-bound} \\|q_l\\|_{C^j(L_n)}\leq C_{L,l,j}e^{A_ln}.$$ The exponent $A_l$ is independent of $j$.
  - Explanation: Angular Taylor coefficients have exponential axis-derivative bounds with rate independent of the derivative order.
- **claim-axis-subexponential** (bound, [source](#stmt-smooth-subexponential)): For every $l,j\geq0$, every $\varepsilon>0$, and every $L\Subset\mathcal B$ there is a constant $C_{L,l,j,\varepsilon}$ such that $$\label{smooth:subexponential-bound} \\|q_l\\|_{C^j(L_n)} \leq C_{L,l,j,\varepsilon}e^{\varepsilon n}.$$
  - Explanation: Every axis derivative of every angular coefficient grows more slowly than any exponential.
- **claim-label-smooth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-smooth-propagation)): The actual endpoint label $q:\mathcal U\to\mathbb R^m$ is smooth.
  - Explanation: The actual endpoint label is smooth on the whole open set, obtained as a limit of finite polynomial approximants in every derivative norm.
- **claim-holonomy-volume-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-smooth-holonomy-volume)): Between two smooth $m$-dimensional local transversals to the weak unstable foliation, its holonomy $h$ satisfies, locally, $$c\,\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(E)\leq\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(h(E))\leq C\,\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(E)$$ for measurable sets $E$, with $0<c\leq C<\infty$.
  - Explanation: Weak unstable holonomy between smooth transversals distorts volume by bounded factors above and below.
- **claim-anosov-splitting-smooth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-smooth-splitting)): In the finite alternative of Theorem <a href="#alg:dichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:dichotomy">46</a>, the actual bundles $E^u$ and $E^s$ are smooth on $SM$.
  - Explanation: In the finite alternative the stable and unstable bundles are smooth on the unit tangent bundle.

        "exact_quote": "Suppose a measurable invertible linear cocycle has an invariant direct sum $E=\\bigoplus_{i=1}^r E_i$, with distinct exponents $\\lambda_1<\\cdots<\\lambda_r$, and each $E_i$ satisfies <a href=\"#nf:lyapunov-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nf:lyapunov-bounds\">[nf:lyapunov-bounds]</a>.         "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be an invertible finite-dimensional measurable cocycle with a single exponent $\\gamma\\ne0$ and estimates <a href=\"#nf:lyapunov-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nf:lyapunov-bounds\">[nf:lyapunov-bounds]</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nf-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "For the raw towers in Lemma <a href=\"#nf:raw-dynamics\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nf:raw-dynamics\">6</a>, at every finite level there are measurable smooth germ coordinates $\\Psi_{v,j}$, tangent to the identity, and weight-preserving polynomial diffeomorphisms $N_{v,j}$ such that $$\\label{nf:conjugacy}\n \\Psi_{fv,j}\\circ G_{v,j}=N_{v,j}\\circ\\Psi_{v,j}.$$ Their linear part is the raw derivative, with its Oseledets grading. The coordinates and normal maps commute with the tower projections. For every fixed state level and every fixed derivative order, their norms and the corresponding inverse norms have measurable tempered bounds on measurable tempered positive radii. At a fixed level the coordinate change is smooth on a forward admissible domain; the estimates at different orders do not require one radius common to the entire infinite tower.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nf-normalization",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nf-fiber-analyticity",
        "exact_quote": "At every fixed state level, the exact coordinate map $\\Psi_{v,j}$ is polynomial in the raw hidden variables at fixed raw base $a$, with smooth coefficients in $a$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nf-finite-weight",
        "exact_quote": "In either normalized tower, the independent coordinates introduced at level $j$ have weights at least $j\\chi_*$ for a fixed $\\chi_*>0$. A prescribed upper weight bound consequently uses only finitely many independent tower coordinates and finitely many monomials. On the array of monomials of a fixed weight $d$, normal substitution has the single exponent $-d$, and inverse substitution has exponent $d$, with two-sided estimates and arbitrarily small exponential slack. These estimates continue to hold for finite tensor arrays and their invariant subquotients, with the corresponding sums and differences of weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hull-main",
        "exact_quote": "At every point of a conull invariant set there are formal affine fields $\\mathcal A^u,\\mathcal A^s$ of horizontal $m$-covectors with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Their values contain $u$ and $w$, respectively. At the center, the exterior product of any element of $\\mathcal A^u$ with any element of $\\mathcal A^s$ equals the fixed nonzero exterior product $u\\wedge w$.*\n\n2.  *The fields are equivariant under $f$, changes of section, and flip, with the same constant and height multipliers as $u,w$. In a flow box their height dependences are $e^{-h\\tau}$ and $e^{h\\tau}$.*\n\n3.  *On each entire typical strong stable or strong unstable leaf, every ambient Taylor coefficient has a smooth representative agreeing almost everywhere with the regular construction. These representatives are holonomic: differentiating along the leaf gives the corresponding higher ambient coefficients. The analogous assertion holds on typical weak leaves, using flow transport.*\n\n4.  *At every finite order the local representatives, their bounds, and positive radii can be chosen measurably. On compact plaque subboxes one may restrict to positive-measure families with uniform finite-order bounds. No common convergence radius for all orders is asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-hull-main",

In a flow box their height dependences are $e^{-h\\tau}$ and $e^{h\\tau}$.*\n\n3.  *On each entire typical strong stable or strong unstable leaf, every ambient Taylor coefficient has a smooth representative agreeing almost everywhere with the regular construction.         "exact_quote": "The smooth germs $V_j(v)$ have compatible positively weighted polynomial parametrizations. Independent parameters can be introduced successively with the state levels.         "exact_quote": "Fix a finite level and monomial array.         "anchor": "stmt-hull-identities",
        "exact_quote": "If a smooth function vanishes on the local actual states, its Taylor series at a typical center vanishes on the formal germ of $V_j$.         "anchor": "stmt-hull-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a weight cutoff $W$ and a target spatial derivative order $k$. There exist integers $r=r(W,k)$ and a constant $C=C(W,k)$ with the following property. Pushing a weight-truncated graph and log-density variation from time $-l$ to the target along the central unstable graph requires only source spatial derivatives through order $r$ at the contracted central preimage.         "exact_quote": "Fix a target norm consisting of graph derivatives through order $k+1$ and log-density derivatives through order $k$, and fix $K>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sym-graded-contact",
        "exact_quote": "Along almost every orbit of $f=\\phi_T$ there are centered formal symplectic section coordinates, with tempered jets at every fixed order, having the following properties. The symplectic form is a constant form $\\omega_0$. If $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_{2m}$ are the signed Lyapunov exponents, counted with multiplicity and measured per iterate of $f$, then $$D=\\sum_i\\lambda_iq_i\\partial_{q_i}$$ is symplectic and every normalized section map commutes with $D$. The coordinates lift strictly with $\\theta_0=\\tfrac12\\iota_R\\omega_0$, where $R=\\sum_iq_i\\partial_{q_i}$, and center height zero. The centered return map has the form $$\\label{sym:graded-return}\n (q,\\tau)\\longmapsto(F(q),\\tau+r(q)),\n \\qquad r(0)=0,\\quad Dr=0.$$ The actual strong stable and unstable Taylor germs are respectively the negative and positive coordinate axes, with their horizontal lifts.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The coordinates lift strictly with $\\theta_0=\\tfrac12\\iota_R\\omega_0$, where $R=\\sum_iq_i\\partial_{q_i}$, and center height zero.           "source_id": "PM-339.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "In the coordinates of Lemma <a href=\"#sym:graded-contact\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sym:graded-contact\">27</a>, $$\\label{sym:dilation-hamiltonian}\n H=-T+\\theta_0(D),\\qquad Z_H=D-T\\partial_\\tau$$ belongs to $\\mathfrak k\\subset\\mathfrak h$. The operator $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ad}}H$ on formal Hamiltonians is $D$ and is semisimple on every finite Taylor quotient, with real weights.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sym-full-projections",
        "exact_quote": "Refine the regular set by a countable conull intersection. For every finite order $r$, the projections of $\\mathfrak h$ and $\\mathfrak k$ to the Hamiltonian $r$-jets have smooth extensions on each entire strong axis through a regular center. Their fibers at every point of that axis are the projections of the full formal solution spaces for the extended background jets there. Projections between successive jet orders are surjective smooth bundle maps. These statements also hold after appending a fixed finite list of linear jet conditions whose matrices are smooth along the axes and equivariant under the return maps.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "The open isotropy $\\mathfrak k$ contains no nonzero ideal of $\\mathfrak h$, and is exactly the coadjoint stabilizer of $\\xi$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sym-axis-transport",
        "exact_quote": "On either entire typical strong axis there are local smooth formal identifications of $(\\mathfrak h,\\mathfrak k,\\xi)$, induced by strict contact coordinate changes. They may be chosen smoothly for a smooth finite-dimensional family of paths on an axis patch. Every intrinsic isotropy-invariant tensor or subspace construction, extended homogeneously as in Proposition <a href=\"#sym:homogeneous-fields\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sym:homogeneous-fields\">33</a>, has smooth full ambient formal jets on that axis, and these jets are holonomic there. The same conclusion holds for a continuous choice from a finite intrinsic list of invariant subspaces after these identifications. Natural choices commute with the dynamics. Flow translation gives the corresponding weak-axis statement, with almost-everywhere agreement at regular centers.",

      "exact_statement": "If $B\\ne0$, let $$P'=\\operatorname{soc}_{\\mathfrak l}(B^*)\\subset P\n \\label{alg:socle}$$ under the transferred representation of Lemma <a href=\"#alg:characteristic-representation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"alg:characteristic-representation\">40</a>; the socle is the sum of all irreducible submodules.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-alg-dichotomy",
        "exact_quote": "At typical centers exactly one of the following alternatives holds, with the same alternative almost everywhere.*\n\n1.  *The symmetry algebra is finite dimensional. There are $\\mathfrak h$-invariant formal fields with center values $E^s$ and $E^u$, obtained from finite isotypic sign choices. Their full ambient jets are smooth and holonomic along both entire typical axes and equivariant under the dynamics.*\n\n2.  *The symmetry algebra is infinite dimensional. The intrinsic proper open subalgebra $\\mathfrak l\\supset\\mathfrak k$ determines an involutive coisotropic formal distribution $L$, whose characteristic distribution is $P=L^\\omega$. It contains an intrinsic nonzero plane $P'$ with nonzero actual stable and unstable parts $P^s,P^u$. These parts are invariant under the full $\\mathfrak u$ action transferred from $B^*$, and all these formal fields have the same whole-axis coherence and equivariance.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-real-iterate-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Forward iterates restricted to uniform local strong stable plaques have uniformly bounded derivatives of every fixed order. On the ambient manifold, for every fixed $j$ there are constants $C_j,K_j$ such that $$\\|f^r\\|_{C^j}+\\|f^{-r}\\|_{C^j}\\le C_j e^{K_jr}\n \\qquad(r\\ge0)$$ in bounded local charts, with the zeroth-order position term omitted. If $D_0\\Subset D_1$ are patches in a strong stable leaf, the sets $f^{-r}D_0$ have uniform positive intrinsic room inside $f^{-r}D_1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-real-iterate-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Forward iterates restricted to uniform local strong stable plaques have uniformly bounded derivatives of every fixed order. The same holds on weak stable plaques with a bounded local flow coordinate. On the ambient manifold, for every fixed $j$ there are constants $C_j,K_j$ such that $$\\|f^r\\|_{C^j}+\\|f^{-r}\\|_{C^j}\\le C_j e^{K_jr}\n \\qquad(r\\ge0)$$ in bounded local charts, with the zeroth-order position term omitted. If $D_0\\Subset D_1$ are patches in a strong stable leaf, the sets $f^{-r}D_0$ have uniform positive intrinsic room inside $f^{-r}D_1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-339.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-real-product-derivatives",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a product of finite matrices of a fixed size, $$A_{r-1}(a)\\cdots A_0(a),$$ on uniform plaque charts. Suppose $\\|A_i\\|\\le M$, with $M\\ge1$, and that $\\|D^j A_i\\|\\le C_j$ for every fixed $j$, uniformly in $i,r$. Then $$\\bigl\\|D^j(A_{r-1}\\cdots A_0)\\bigr\\|\n    \\le C'_j(r+1)^j M^r\n    \\le C''_j e^{(\\log M+1)r}.$$ The exponential rate is independent of $j$.         "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-real-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "On uniform coordinate balls with a fixed amount of interior room, for integers $0<j<r$, $$\\label{real:interpolation-inequality}\n \\|D^j a\\|_{C^0}\n \\le C_{j,r}\\left(\n   \\|a\\|_{C^0}^{1-j/r}\\|a\\|_{C^r}^{j/r}\n     +\\|a\\|_{C^0}\\right).$$ In particular, if $\\|a_n\\|_{C^0}\\le C e^{-\\beta n}$ and $\\|a_n\\|_{C^r}\\le C_r e^{A n}$, then its $j$th derivatives are bounded by a constant times $$e^{-\\{\\beta(1-j/r)-Aj/r\\}n}+e^{-\\beta n}.$$ The statement holds componentwise for tensors in uniformly bounded frames.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-real-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "On uniform coordinate balls with a fixed amount of interior room, for integers $0<j<r$, $$\\label{real:interpolation-inequality}\n \\|D^j a\\|_{C^0}\n \\le C_{j,r}\\left(\n   \\|a\\|_{C^0}^{1-j/r}\\|a\\|_{C^r}^{j/r}\n     +\\|a\\|_{C^0}\\right).$$ In particular, if $\\|a_n\\|_{C^0}\\le C e^{-\\beta n}$ and $\\|a_n\\|_{C^r}\\le C_r e^{A n}$, then its $j$th derivatives are bounded by a constant times $$e^{-\\{\\beta(1-j/r)-Aj/r\\}n}+e^{-\\beta n}.$$ The statement holds componentwise for tensors in uniformly bounded frames.",
          "source_id": "PM-339.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-real-good-points",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $k$ and a sufficiently small geometric radius $\\rho>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-real-actualization",
        "exact_quote": "For the patches and frame in <a href=\"#real:pulled-jet\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"real:pulled-jet\">[real:pulled-jet]</a>, there is a smooth map $$F:D\\times\\mathbb R^{d_u}\\longrightarrow S\\widetilde M$$ whose Taylor series in $y$ at zero is $\\mathcal F$, such that $$F(a,0)=a,\\qquad D_yF(a,0)=Y(a),\\qquad F(a,y)\\in W^u(a).$$ The construction depends only on the initial vector $Y(a)y$ and is compatible on overlapping patches and under $f$.*\n\n\n\n*More precisely, put $$V_{n,R}(s,y)=\\sum_{k=1}^R G_{n,k}(s)[y^{\\otimes k}],\n \\qquad\n F_{n,R}(a,y)=f^n\\exp_{f^{-n}a}V_{n,R}(f^{-n}a,y).$$ On every compact parameter set and compactly contained base patch, the following holds. For every fixed $j$ and $L>0$, all sufficiently large fixed $R$ give $$\\label{real:fast-approximation}\n \\|F-F_{n,R}\\|_{C^j}\\le C_{R,j,L}e^{-Ln},$$ and, after applying $f^{-n}$ and taking the logarithm based at $f^{-n}a$, $$\\label{real:fast-backward}\n \\left\\|\\log_{f^{-n}a}(f^{-n}F(a,y))\n       -V_{n,R}(f^{-n}a,y)\\right\\|_{C^j(a,y)}\n       \\le C_{R,j,L}e^{-Ln}.$$ Norms comparing nearby points use common bounded charts.",

For every fixed $j$ and $L>0$, all sufficiently large fixed $R$ give $$\\label{real:fast-approximation}\n \\|F-F_{n,R}\\|_{C^j}\\le C_{R,j,L}e^{-Ln},$$ and, after applying $f^{-n}$ and taking the logarithm based at $f^{-n}a$, $$\\label{real:fast-backward}\n \\left\\|\\log_{f^{-n}a}(f^{-n}F(a,y))\n       -V_{n,R}(f^{-n}a,y)\\right\\|_{C^j(a,y)}\n       \\le C_{R,j,L}e^{-Ln}.$$ Norms comparing nearby points use common bounded charts.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-real-normal-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Q$ be the formal projection onto the perpendicular of $P^s$ for a smooth background metric. At regular centers, and on their entire typical unstable axes, use its smooth coherent coefficient extensions. For each fixed pair of integers $N,r$, one can choose a positive-measure set of centers with the following uniform property: on a fixed small unstable plaque through the center, there is a smooth ambient matrix field $\\widehat Q$ which equals the actual annihilating projection on the plaque, has the prescribed ambient Taylor jet through order $N$ at the center, and has uniformly bounded $C^r$ norm in a neighborhood of that plaque.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-339.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-smooth-tangency",
        "exact_quote": "In the finite alternative, the positive-$D$ invariant formal plane field is tangent to the entire formal central unstable plaque. Consequently, on each entire typical strong stable leaf, all finite Taylor jets of the actual weak unstable plaques are smooth functions of their crossing point. They have holonomic full formal extensions there. The same statements hold on the weak stable leaf generated by that strong stable leaf.",
        "source_id": "PM-339.1"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **tempered function** ([source](#stmt-nf-tempered)): A measurable function $C\geq1$ on the regular set is *tempered* if $$\lim_{\|n\|\to\infty}\frac{\log C(f^n v)}{\|n\|}=0.$$ A positive radius is tempered when its reciprocal, increased to at least one, is tempered. An estimate with arbitrarily small exponential slack means that for every $\epsilon>0$ its prefactor can be chosen tempered, while its exponential rate increases by at most $\epsilon$.
- **normalized Liouville measure** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $m_L$ for normalized Liouville measure;

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-entropy-forms** [claim](#stmt-pre-entropy-forms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-entropy-forms): Gibbs estimates for two equilibrium potentials, Livsic cohomology, then conormal forms built from the Riccati tensor -> transported conormal volume forms with exponential flow scaling
- **step-normal-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-nf-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nf-normalization): Solve homological equations degree by degree after Lyapunov block splitting, then telescope to an exact smooth change -> weight-preserving polynomial normal forms
- **step-formal-fields** [claim](#stmt-hull-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hull-measurability-2): Zariski closures of actual states, pure-weight evaluation by transported returns, spans of conormal alternatives -> coherent formal affine fields on both foliations
- **step-symmetry-trace** [claim](#stmt-sym-effective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sym-effective): Formal strict contact symmetries, signed dilation and entropy trace identity -> effective symplectic homogeneous algebra with trace identity
- **step-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-alg-dichotomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-dichotomy): Cartan-Guillemin structure of minimal ideals, equicontinuity subalgebra, positivity of the trace on reductive quotients -> finite or infinite-dimensional alternative with sign fields
- **step-exclude-infinite** [claim](#stmt-real-infinite-excluded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-infinite-excluded): Proper stable ray, actualized mixed rectangle, Busemann function contradiction at the ideal boundary -> infinite alternative impossible
- **step-smooth-label** [claim](#stmt-smooth-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-smooth-propagation): Interpolate fast low-order approximation of polynomial approximants against slow high-order growth -> smooth endpoint label
- **step-smooth-bundles** [claim](#stmt-smooth-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-smooth-splitting): Bounded holonomy volume distortion makes the label a submersion; kernel gives the bundles -> smooth stable and unstable bundles
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-smooth-splitting-3): Chain of results forward; locally symmetric converse via constant Riccati root trace -> entropy equality iff parallel curvature

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-entropy-equality-iff-symmetric**: For a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension at least three with strictly negative sectional curvature, equality of Liouville entropy and topological entropy of the time-one geodesic flow holds exactly when the metric is locally symmetric.
- **inv-smooth-splitting-forces-symmetry**: Global smoothness of the stable and unstable bundles on the unit tangent bundle forces local symmetry of the metric.
- **inv-entropy-equality-cohomology**: Under entropy equality the unstable expansion rate equals the common entropy plus a flow derivative of a Hölder function.
- **inv-algebraic-dichotomy**: At typical centers exactly one of two alternatives holds: the formal symmetry algebra is finite dimensional or it is infinite dimensional, and the alternative is the same almost everywhere.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-surface-case**: Apply the theorem to a surface, where the base dimension is two. -> The statement does not cover that case; the argument is stated only for dimension at least three.
- **anti-nonnegative-curvature**: Use entropy equality to conclude local symmetry for a metric that is flat or has curvature of mixed sign. -> Uniform hyperbolicity of the geodesic flow and every structural step rely on strictly negative curvature, so no conclusion follows.
- **anti-assume-smooth-splitting**: Assume the stable and unstable bundles are smooth and cite the rigidity proposition as if it proved entropy rigidity. -> The proposition is conditional; smoothness of the bundles is the hard conclusion obtained from entropy equality in the main argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper's results are rigidity and existence theorems about smooth geodesic flows: entropy equality implies local symmetry, via formal symmetry algebras, smooth bundles and ergodic-theoretic limits. No finite procedure with explicit steps is stated that computes an object from finite input, so no reference implementation or oracle is meaningful.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a rigidity statement for geodesic flows on closed manifolds and gives no finite procedure a program could run. Its smoothness-to-symmetry step is a theorem, not an API or routine. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The geodesic flow and the unstable expansion rate appear only inside a proof of metric rigidity. No simulation scheme or discretisation is given, so a physics or game engine cannot be derived from the text. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main output is an existence dichotomy for formal symmetry algebras at typical centers, with exactly one alternative holding. No instrument or checker is constructed; the algebraic objects are formal and measurable only in the centre. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The recurrence solution in the homological lemma is a convergent series argument along regular orbits, with no complexity statement or finite stopping rule. The overall argument is existential and uses countable refinements of conull sets, so no algorithm is extractable. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The formal affine fields and jet towers are analytic objects living on a conull invariant set. The paper defines no storable representation, indexing scheme or update rule for them, so no data structure follows. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only finite statement about weights says that new coordinates at each level carry weights growing with the level. That is a structural bound inside a proof, with no kernel, dataflow or numeric format to map onto hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The symmetry-algebra results concern coadjoint stabilizers in a dynamical setting and have no hardness assumption, adversary model or protocol. Nothing in the paper links to a security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Statements about good points in stable disks are measure-theoretic existence results for jet bounds, not statistical estimation or learning guarantees. No data, loss or generalisation claim is present. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Pressure zero and the equilibrium-state property are variational facts about a potential, not a feasible-set optimisation problem with a solver or certificate. Maximal entropy is characterised, not computed. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only notion of propagation is regularity moving along leaves of a foliation. There are no nodes, messages, faults or consensus, so nothing maps to a distributed protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a pure differential-geometric rigidity theorem and its value is mathematical: it turns an entropy equality into local symmetry for closed, strictly negatively curved manifolds of dimension at least three. It supplies no engineered artefact in any further domain. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dimension-lower-bound** (hypothesis): The equivalence between entropy equality and parallel curvature is stated only for base dimension at least three; the surface case is treated by a different argument and is outside this theorem. Blocked use: ['Applying the equivalence to a closed surface', 'Citing the theorem as a classification statement in base dimension two']
- **limit-strict-negative-curvature** (hypothesis): Every step relies on every sectional curvature being strictly negative, which supplies the uniformly hyperbolic geodesic flow; weaker curvature conditions are not covered. Blocked use: ['Using entropy equality to conclude local symmetry for a flat metric', 'Using the theorem for metrics of mixed or non-strict curvature sign']
- **limit-closed-connected-manifold** (hypothesis): The base is a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold; compactness is used for uniform plaque bounds, ergodic-theoretic recurrence and finite covers. Blocked use: ['Applying the argument to non-compact complete manifolds', 'Applying it to manifolds with boundary or to billiard-type systems']
- **limit-orientation-reduction** (edge_case): The argument passes to an oriented finite cover and assumes orientability until the end; the conclusion for the original manifold uses that local symmetry descends through the cover. Blocked use: ['Using orientation-dependent conormal volume forms on a non-orientable base without passing to the cover']
- **limit-entropy-equality-assumed** (hypothesis): The cohomology identity for the unstable expansion rate and the transported conormal volume forms are available only under the assumption that Liouville and topological entropy coincide. Blocked use: ['Using the conormal forms or the cohomology identity for a metric whose entropies differ']
- **limit-smoothness-is-conditional** (formal_scope): The classical rigidity step is conditional on global smoothness of the stable and unstable bundles; smoothness is not a general property of negatively curved geodesic flows and is what the main argument must establish. Blocked use: ['Citing the rigidity proposition without establishing smoothness of both bundles', 'Assuming smooth invariant splittings for an arbitrary strictly negatively curved metric']
- **limit-normal-coordinates-measurable-only** (formal_scope): The normal coordinates are only measurable in their centers and are never differentiated with respect to the center; bounds hold at each fixed finite level and order. Blocked use: ['Differentiating normal coordinates or the formal towers in the center variable', 'Assuming one radius or one bound valid for the entire infinite tower']
- **limit-no-convergent-series** (formal_scope): Finite weight arrays do not identify an infinite formal series with a convergent spatial germ; only finite cutoffs and order-by-order statements are available. Blocked use: ['Evaluating the formal fields or jet towers as convergent series at a nonzero point', 'Reading finite-weight estimates as analyticity in the base or center']
- **limit-no-common-radius** (edge_case): The hull construction gives measurable local representatives order by order; no single convergence radius is claimed for all orders at once. Blocked use: ['Taking a uniform neighbourhood on which the formal affine fields converge for every order simultaneously']
- **limit-algebra-over-complex-numbers** (formal_scope): The structure theory of the symmetry algebra is carried out after complexification and linearly compact topologies; real structures are recovered afterwards, and the alternative holds only almost everywhere at typical centers. Blocked use: ['Applying the finite or infinite dichotomy at a specific non-typical point', 'Using the classification input without the complexified, formal-topology setting']
- **limit-almost-everywhere-agreement** (edge_case): Regular data agree with the actual objects only leaf-almost-everywhere; an identity at every point of a leaf has to be obtained separately by smooth or continuous extension. Blocked use: ['Reading almost-everywhere agreement as a pointwise identity on a null set of centers', 'Evaluating regular measurable data at non-typical points of a leaf']
- **limit-prefactors-not-uniform** (approximation): The pulled-back jet decay has an exponential rate independent of the fixed orders, but the prefactors depend on the orders, the initial patches and the frame; no uniform prefactor bound is claimed. Blocked use: ['Summing the decay estimate over all derivative orders or degrees with a single constant', 'Treating the constants as effective numerical bounds']
- **limit-constants-depend-on-metric** (unknown): All curvature-dependent constants depend on the given compact metric and are not made explicit or effective in the paper. Blocked use: ['Quoting numerical values for contraction, decay or distortion constants', 'Claiming constants uniform over families of metrics']
